# Plan: Vedic Sanskrit A1 word lists, sourced

Task VSA-WORDS of [Deck-Work-Queue-VSA](Deck-Work-Queue-VSA.md), written 2026-11-16 for the Haiku builders of the A1 word units of [Plan-Vedic-Path](Plan-Vedic-Path.md). Every word below was found in a verse of the Rigveda on a page that was read, and glossed from Griffith's translation of the same verse. Nothing was typed from memory into a deck field: a builder copies the headword, the line and the Griffith English from this page, and takes `reading` and `ipa` from the tool (VSA-110), never from here.

**Result.** All 23 word units of A1 (units 3, 4, 5, 6, 11, 12, 14, 15, 16, 18, 19, 20, 21, 23, 24, 25, 26, 27, 28, 30, 31, 32, 33) reach their planned size: **290 words** (the plan's 290 for these units; with the 10 written words of unit 1 that is the A1 total of 300). No unit is short. Grammar and script units carry no words and are not on this page.

## How a word got on this page

1. **Corpus read.** On 2026-11-16 all 1,028 Rigveda hymn pages of sa.wikisource (title `ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं <book>.<hymn>`, Devanagari digits) and all 1,028 Griffith pages of en.wikisource (`The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book <N>/Hymn <M>`) were fetched through the MediaWiki API in batches of 40 titles, honouring `retry-after` on HTTP 429. 10,099 verses were parsed from the `<poem>` blocks of the Sanskrit pages (the text proper, not Sayana's commentary, which carries accent marks; the poem blocks carry none, checked: no U+0951, U+0952 or U+1CD0 to U+1CFF in any line below). Four Griffith pages (1.1, 1.32, 4.27, 5.1) are page-scan transclusions without verse text and were not used.
2. **Candidates** were the author's choice of high-frequency words that fit the unit theme (pronouns, family, numbers, gods, `soma`, `indra`, ...). A candidate is only a search key; it counts for nothing until step 3.
3. **Accepted only if** (a) the headword occurs as a written word of the Sanskrit line, exactly, or with the usual anusvara for final m (the corpus writes ं, the headword म्) or -o for -aḥ before a sonant, or fused in one written word with its neighbour (5 cases, marked); (b) the Sanskrit hymn and the Griffith hymn have the same number of verses, so the verse number lines up; (c) Griffith's verse of the same number contains an English word of the gloss (a keyword test). The line chosen is one line of the verse (a half verse, 25 words at most; all are 2 to 11 words), and the English given is Griffith's **whole verse**, since Griffith translates by verse, not by line. A card's example uses the Sanskrit line; its `native` line is written by the builder from the Griffith verse shown.
4. **`check`.** `strong` means the gloss is a content word whose English reflex was found in Griffith's verse. `weak` marks pronouns, particles and a few broad words where the keyword test proves little; the line is still a real attestation of the word, but the gloss rests on the word's ordinary sense, and the builder keeps the gloss as given and does not widen it.
5. **Glosses** are short, are the author's, and were each confirmed by the English of the Griffith verse printed with the word. The page title for every gloss is the Griffith page named in the row (Whitney was used for grammar notes only, below).
6. **Headword forms.** The headword is the dictionary form (nominative singular, or for verbs the third singular present) when that form occurs verbatim; where only another form of the word occurs in a checked verse, the headword is that attested form and the row says `inflected: true` in the YAML. For a Vedic word the card then teaches that form, with the stem in `bases` as in `decks/vsn/vsn-first-words.yaml` (a noun that is its own base gets `bases: "self"`, an inflected word gets its stem; a verb's base is its root, as for वद् and ज्ञा there).
7. **Grammar notes.** Each is a feature found on a Whitney page read on 2026-11-16 (en.wikisource, `Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter I` to `XVIII` fetched; the chapter and section number are given). Where Whitney writes accents, the notes keep them in the Latin transliteration only; no accent mark enters a Devanagari target (VSA-100 policy).

Overlap with the written cards was removed: अहम्, त्वम्, आपः, अग्निः, देवः, न, एकः, द्वौ, गौः, अश्वः and the phrasebook words (including कः, किम्, क्व, कियत्, ऋतम्, यज्ञः, मित्रः) are not repeated.

## Caveats the architect should read

- The Sanskrit text is sa.wikisource's transcription, which names no print edition on the pages read; the Griffith pages are the 1896 edition as transcribed (hymn 10.34, which Plan-Culture-Vedic records as a modern 2006 revision, is excluded from this page's corpus).
- 44 of 290 words are `weak` (see above). They are listed so a reviewer can look at them first.
- Griffith's verse numbering was trusted only where the verse counts of the two pages agree. A verse could still be numbered one off inside such a hymn; the keyword test would usually catch that, but not always for pronouns. The reviewer of the units checks the weak rows against the Griffith page.
- Unit 6 holds three to twelve (eleven is attested only as the Vedic nominative plural एकादशासः in RV 9.92.4, 'the Thrice-Eleven'); thirteen to nineteen were not searched, since the ten planned words are filled; twenty is `विंशतिः` in unit 31. Unit 31's `विंशतिः` is twenty, so unit 6 does not need it.
- The directions unit (32) has no compass-point nouns of the classical kind; Griffith's English shows 'eastward', 'southward', 'before', 'regions', which are what the five words mean in the verses read.
- Three verb headwords are first-person or middle forms as attested (हुवे, दुहे, ईळे, and सचते, मन्यते, which are third-person middle); they are listed as the attested forms with their roots, and a builder who wants the third singular active asks the architect, never writes one unsourced.


## Unit 3: `vsn-questions` (questions), 5 of 5 words

| # | Headword | Stem or root | Part of speech | Gloss | Attested at (sa.wikisource page, verse; line no. in the verse) | The line | Griffith's English (whole verse, en.wikisource page) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | कदा | कदा | adverb | when? | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.२५ verse 5, line 1; attested as कदा | कदा क्षत्रश्रियं नरमा वरुणं करामहे | When shall we bring, to be appeased, the Hero, Lord of warrior might, Him, the far-seeing Varuna? [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 25] |
| 2 | कथा | कथा | adverb | how? | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.१२० verse 1, line 2; attested as कथा | कथा विधात्यप्रचेताः | ASVINS, what praise may win your grace? Who may be pleasing to you both? How shall the ignorant worship you? [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 120] |
| 3 | कुह | कुह | adverb | where? | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.४६ verse 9, line 2; attested as कुह | स्वं वव्रिं कुह धित्सथः | Kanvas, the drops are in the heaven; the wealth is at the waters' place: Where will ye manifest your form? [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 46] |
| 4 | कति | कति | numeral | how many? | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १०.८८ verse 18, line 1; attested as कति | कत्यग्नयः कति सूर्यासः कत्युषासः कत्यु स्विदापः | How many are the Fires and Suns in number? What is the number of the Dawns and Waters? Not jestingly I speak to you, O Fathers. Sages, I ask you this for information. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 10/Hymn 88] |
| 5 | कस्मै | क | pronoun | for whom? | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ५.५३ verse 2, line 2; attested as कस्मै | कस्मै सस्रुः सुदासे अन्वापय इळाभिर्वृष्टयः सह | Who, when they stood upon their cars, hath heard them tell the way they went? Who was the bounteous man to whom their kindred rains flowed down with food of sacrifice? [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 5/Hymn 53] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- The interrogative stem is k- (ka, ki, ku); nominative masculine kás, neuter kím, and the Veda also has the pronominally regular neuter kád. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VII, section 504 a.)
- káti, kathā́, kadā́, kúha, kútra and kútas are made from the roots ka, ki, ku. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VII, section 505.)
- Added ca, caná, cid, ápi or vā turn the interrogative into an indefinite ('any one'). (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VII, section 507.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): कुतः कुत्र.

[The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 2/Hymn 18] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- The first and second person pronouns have no gender; nominative singular ahám and tvám, plural vayám and yūyám. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VII, section 491.)
- The demonstrative ta- has nominative sás (m.), sā́ (f.), tát (n.); sás loses its s before any consonant (sa dadarça), and so does eṣás. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VII, section 495 a and III 176 a.)
- ya- is the relative only: yás, yát, yā́. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VII, section 508-509.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): जनाः त्वा.

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- Nouns in -ṛ are mostly agent nouns in -tṛ, plus 'a few nouns of relationship'; the model pitṛ́ gives nominative pitā́, accusative pitáram, genitive pitúr, and svásṛ gives svásā. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 369.)
- u-stems (çátru, dhenú, mádhu) have nominative -us (çátrus, dhenús); u-stems are the declension of nouns like these. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 341.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): पत्नी वधूः नप्ता जामिः नारी पुमान् अपत्यम् पितरः मातरः भ्रातरः पुत्राः पत्न्यः.

Ten on the fleecy height, themselves, self-prompted, and seven fresh rivers, brighten and adorn thee. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 9/Hymn 92] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- The first ten cardinals are éka, dvá, trí, catúr, páñca, ṣáṣ, saptá, aṣṭá, náva, dáça. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VI, section 475.)
- Gender is distinguished only by the first four numerals; dva is dual only. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VI, section 482.)
- Numbers from 5 to 19 have no gender and show the bare stem in nominative-accusative; aṣṭá has an older longer form aṣṭā́. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VI, section 483.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): एकादश अष्ट तिस्रः द्वे द्वा.

| 1 | पश्यति | पश् | verb | sees | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ९.१० verse 9, line 2; attested as पश्यति | सूरः पश्यति चक्षसा | The Sun with his dear eye beholds that quarter of the heavens which priests Have placed within the sacred cell. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 9/Hymn 10] |
| 2 | शृणोति | श्रु | verb | hears | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.३७ verse 13, line 2; attested as शृणोति | शृणोति कश्चिदेषाम् | The while the Maruts pass along, they talk together on the way: Doth any hear them as they speak? [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 37] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- The present-system holds the present indicative, subjunctive (mostly lost later), optative, imperative, participle and the imperfect. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IX, section 599.)
- brū 'speak' takes the union-vowel ī before consonant endings: brávīmi, brávīṣi, brávīti. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IX, section 632.)
- Verbs of the reduplicating class prefix a shortened reduplication: dadā- from dā, bibhṛ- from bhṛ. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IX, section 643.)
- han 'smite' loses its n before a consonant ending and its a before a vowel, so its forms differ from the root. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IX, section 637.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): करोति विन्दति ब्रवीति.

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- Final -as before a sonant becomes -o (índro for índras), and the following short a is lost only occasionally in the Veda. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter III, section 175 a, 135 c.)
- Vedic a-stems have nominative plural -āsas (devā́sas) and instrumental plural -ebhis (devébhis) beside -āis. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 330 c.)
- The Vedic dual of a-stem masculines can be devā́ (also devāú). (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 330 b.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): आदित्याः रुद्राः अश्विना पूषन् त्वष्टः वृत्रहा.

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- uṣás 'dawn' has long ā in the strong cases: uṣā́sam, uṣā́sā, uṣā́sas. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 415.)
- dív 'sky, day' has forms divā́ (instrumental), diví (locative), divás (genitive). (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 361.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): अन्तरिक्षम् चन्द्रमाः वृष्टिः सिन्धुः नदी रश्मिः अभ्रम् ओषधयः चन्द्रमसा रश्मिभिः विद्युतः.

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- Adverbs in -tra come from pronominal stems: átra, tátra, yátra, kútra. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVI, section 1099.)
- Adverbs in -thā: táthā, yáthā, kathā́, itthā́. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVI, section 1101.)
- The ablative is used as an adverb: dūrā́t 'afar', nīcā́t 'below', paçcā́t 'behind'. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVI, section 1114.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): पुरा सदा शश्वत् सनत् अथ एव इत् तदा पश्चा नीचा.

The Hero is renowned for gathering riches. Great is the Gods' supreme and sole dominion. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 3/Hymn 55] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- Adjectives are inflected exactly like nouns of the same final. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 322.)
- Comparatives and superlatives in -īyas and -iṣṭha (çréyas, çréṣṭha), or with -tara and -tama. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 470 c, 471.)
- bhū́ri 'abundant' is among the words in -ri, with sūrí 'patron' and úsri 'dawn'. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVII, section 1191.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): बहुः पुरुः भद्रः उरुः अरुषः ऊर्ध्वः भद्रा पुराणः पुराणम् नूतनः सुखम् तविषः अरुणः सुशेवः अजरः दक्षः वरिष्ठः वरिष्ठम् तुविः.

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- havís n. 'oblation' is a model of the neuter s-stems (nominative-accusative havís, instrumental havíṣā). (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 414.)
- Exclamations used at the sacrifice, mostly in -ā, include svā́hā, svadhā́ and váṣaṭ. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XV, section 1091.)
- From su 'press' the present participle is sunvánt (active) and sunvāná (middle). (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IX, section 705.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): घृतम् अध्वरः यजमानः अध्वर्युः उद्गाता धूमः आज्यम् क्रतुः अरणिः ऋत्विजम् ऋत्विक् इन्धनम् समित् समिधः हवनम् अग्नीध्रः आहुतिः इष्टिः आज्येन ईळ्यः स्रुवः यूपः यूपम् चमसः ग्रावाणः द्रोणे द्रोणम्.

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- sárva 'all', víçva 'all, every' and éka 'one' are declined like pronominal adjectives. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VII, section 524.)
- bhū́ri 'abundant' is among the words in -ri. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVII, section 1191.)
- Multiplicative adverbs from numerals: dvís, trís, catúr; sakṛ́t 'once' is a compound. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVI, section 1105.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): सर्वः पूर्णः यावत् तावत् अर्धम् अल्पः कृत्स्नः विश्वे मात्रा उभे एकः सकृत् यावान् तावान् विश्वा बहु सर्वा सर्वान् सर्वाः यावतः तावतः.

| 6 | शरदः | शरद् | noun (f) | autumn, year | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १०.८५ verse 39, line 2; attested as शरदः | दीर्घायुरस्या यः पतिर्जीवाति शरदः शतम् | Agni hath given the bride again with splendour and with ample life. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 10/Hymn 90] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- áhan n. 'day' uses áhar or áhas in the middle cases and the nominative; áhnā and áhni are the weak forms. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 430 a.)
- dív 'sky, day' has the instrumental divā́ (also locative diví, genitive divás). (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 361.)
- náktam 'by night' is an adverb in -am. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVI, section 1111.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): रात्रिः ऋतुः हेमन्तः अहानि मासाः दिनम् शरद् संवत्सरः वत्सरः.

| 3 | पश्चात् | पश्चात् | adverb | behind, afterwards | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १०.८७ verse 21, line 1; attested as पश्चात्पुरस्तादधरादुदक्तात्कविः | पश्चात्पुरस्तादधरादुदक्तात्कविः काव्येन परि पाहि राजन् | From rear, from front, from under, from above us, O King, protect us as a Sage with wisdom. Guard to old age thy friend, O Friend, Eternal: O Agni, as Immortal, guard us mortals. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 10/Hymn 87] |
| 4 | दूरे | दूर | adverb | far | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.१३२ verse 6, line 2; attested as दूरे | दूरे चत्ताय च्छन्त्सद्गहनं यदिनक्षत् | Indra and Parvata, our champions in the fight, drive ye away each man who fain would war with us, drive him far from us with the bolt. Welcome to him concealed afar shall he the lair that he hath found. So may the Render rend our foes on every side, rend them, O Hero, everywhere. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 132] |
| 5 | मध्ये | मध्य | adverb | in the middle | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १०.५ verse 1, line 2; attested as मध्ये | सिषक्त्यूधर्निण्योरुपस्थ उत्सस्य मध्ये निहितं पदं वेः | HE only is the Sea, holder of treasures: born many a time he views the hearts within us. He hides him in the secret couple's bosom. The Bird dwells in the middle of the fountain. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 10/Hymn 5] |
| 6 | परः | पर | adjective | far, beyond | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ५.१७ verse 2, line 2; attested as परो | तं नाकं चित्रशोचिषं मन्द्रं परो मनीषया | Near him thou seemest mightier still in native glory, set to hold Apart yon flame-hued vault of heaven, lovely beyond the thought of man. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 5/Hymn 17] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- The ablative of adjective stems is used adverbially: dūrā́t, nīcā́t, paçcā́t. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVI, section 1114.)
- purā́ (and purás) mean 'forward from' and hence 'before'. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IV, section 293 b.)
- purás 'in front, forward' is used as a prefix with a few roots, especially kṛ, dhā, i. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XV, section 1078.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): उपरि अधः अभि समीपे अन्तिके.

[The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 133] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- The root class has no class-sign; the primary endings are added to the root itself. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IX, section 611-612.)
- The roots īḍ and īç insert a union-vowel i before some endings. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IX, section 630.)
- The root duh has special irregularities in the older language. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IX, section 635.)

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- gír f. 'song' is a monosyllabic root noun; ṛ́c 'verse' likewise. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 392, 383.)
- dhī́ f. 'thought' is the model of monosyllabic ī-stems. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 351.)
- Neuter s-stems such as vácas 'speech', mánas, cákṣas are derived with -as. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVII, section 1151.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): वाक् ऋक् उक्थम् गाथा गिरः सामानि यजुः मन्त्रम् ऋचः वाचम् शस्त्रम् मन्मा.

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- dhenú f. 'cow' is the model u-stem feminine (nominative dhenús). (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 341.)
- The Veda has ukṣán 'bull' and vṛ́ṣan 'bull' as n-stems. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 426.)
- kṣīrá n. 'milk' belongs to the nouns in -ra. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVII, section 1188.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): गोपः व्रजः अविः अश्वा सुरभिः उक्षाणम् पशवः गावः गोमत् अश्वाः अश्वम् गोष्ठम् गवे अनडुत् पशून् पशुम् अविम् अवयः गोपाम् ऋषभः गोमान् अश्ववान् धेनवः उक्षणः वृषणः गाः गोभिः हयः हयम् स्थूरी गवाम् अनड्वान् वत्सम् वत्साः धेनुम्.

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- cákṣus n. 'eye' is declined like mánas (instrumental cákṣuṣā). (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 414.)
- ātmán m. 'soul, self' is a model n-stem: nominative ātmā́. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 424.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): पादः अक्षि कर्णः नासा बाहुः अङ्गम् जङ्घा ओष्ठः केशः अस्थि उदरम् पाणिः तनूः अङ्गुलिः हस्ताभ्याम् कर्णौ लोम त्वक् श्रोत्रम् रक्तम् नाभिः पादौ वक्षः.

[The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 2/Hymn 5] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- mádhu n. 'honey' is the model neuter u-stem. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 341.)
- kṣīrá 'milk' and súrā 'liquor' are among the nouns in -ra. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVII, section 1188.)

## Unit 28: `vsn-war` (battle, weapons, enemies, heroes), 14 of 14 words

| # | Headword | Stem or root | Part of speech | Gloss | Attested at (sa.wikisource page, verse; line no. in the verse) | The line | Griffith's English (whole verse, en.wikisource page) |

| 1 | वज्रः | वज्र | noun (m) | thunderbolt, mace | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ९.४७ verse 3, line 1; attested as वज्रः | आत्सोम इन्द्रियो रसो वज्रः सहस्रसा भुवत् | Soon as his song of praise is born, the Soma, Indra's juice, becomes A thousand-winning thunderbolt. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 9/Hymn 47] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- vájra 'thunderbolt' and çū́ra 'hero' are among the nouns in -ra. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVII, section 1188.)
- Final -as before a sonant becomes -o. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter III, section 175 a.)

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- rā́jan m. 'king' is a model n-stem: nominative rā́jā. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 424.)
- The vocative is accented: rā́jan soma 'O king Soma!'. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter IV, section 314.)
- Vedic n-stems include vṛ́ṣan and ukṣán. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 426.)

The Bold One left malignities behind him, wise, in wild joy of Soma, left the foolish. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 4/Hymn 26] |
| 5 | षष्टिः | षष्टि | numeral (f) | sixty | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.१२६ verse 3, line 2; attested as षष्टिः | षष्टिः सहस्रमनु गव्यमागात्सनत्कक्षीवाँ अभिपित्वे अह्नाम् | Horses of dusky colour stood beside me, ten chariots, Svanaya's gift, with mares to draw them. Kine numbering sixty thousand followed after. Kaksivan gained them when the days were closing. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 126] |
| 6 | पञ्चाशत् | पञ्चाशत् | numeral (f) | fifty | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ४.१६ verse 13, line 2; attested as पञ्चाशत्कृष्णा | पञ्चाशत्कृष्णा नि वपः सहस्रात्कं न पुरो जरिमा वि दर्दः | Thou to the son of Vidathin, Rjisvan, gavest up mighty Mrgaya and Pipru. Thou smotest down the swarthy fifty thousand, and rentest forts as age consumes a garment. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 4/Hymn 16] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- Tens and higher members: viṅçatí 20, triṅçát 30, catvāriṅçát 40, pañcāçát 50, ṣaṣṭí 60, saptatí 70, açītí 80, navatí 90, çatá 100, sahásra 1000, ayúta 10,000. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VI, section 475.)
- Multiples are made by putting the multiplied number in the plural: páñca çatā́ni 'five hundreds', trī́ṇi sahásrāṇi 'three thousands'. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter VI, section 480.)

Candidates tried and dropped (no verse passed the three tests): त्रिंशत् नवतिः सप्ततिः चत्वारिंशत् अशीतिः विंशतिम् षष्टिम् अयुता सहस्राणि शता शतानि सहस्रा.

| 1 | प्राची | प्राञ्च् | adjective (f) | eastward, east (Griffith: "Eastward") | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ५.२८ verse 1, line 2; attested as प्राची | एति प्राची विश्ववारा नमोभिर्देवाँ ईळाना हविषा घृताची | AGNI inflamed hath sent to heaven his lustre: he shines forth widely turning unto Morning. | 2 | दक्षिणतः | दक्षिणतस् | adverb | on the right, southward | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १०.१५ verse 6, line 1; attested as दक्षिणतो | आच्या जानु दक्षिणतो निषद्येमं यज्ञमभि गृणीत विश्वे | Bowing your bended knees and seated southward, accept this sacrifice of ours with favour. Punish us not for any sin, O Fathers, which we through human frailty have committed. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 10/Hymn 15] |
| 3 | पुरस्तात् | पुरस्तात् | adverb | in front, before | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ६.१९ verse 9, line 1; attested as पुरस्तात् | आ ते शुष्मो वृषभ एतु पश्चादोत्तरादधरादा पुरस्तात् | Let thine heroic strength come from behind us, before us, from above us or below us. From every side may it approach us, Indra. Give us the glory of the realm of splendour. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 6/Hymn 19] |
| 4 | प्राचीनम् | प्राचीन | adjective (n) | eastward, forward | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.१८८ verse 4, line 1; attested as प्राचीनं | प्राचीनं बर्हिरोजसा सहस्रवीरमस्तृणन् | To seat a thousand Heroes they eastward have strewn the grass with might, Whereon, Adityas, ye shine forth. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 188] |
| 5 | दिशः | दिश् | noun (f) | regions, directions | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ९.११४ verse 3, line 1; attested as दिशो | सप्त दिशो नानासूर्याः सप्त होतार ऋत्विजः | Seven regions have their several Suns; the ministering priests are seven; Seven are the Aditya Deities,-with these, O Soma, guard thou us. Flow, Indu, flow for Indra's sake. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 9/Hymn 114] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- Prepositional adverbs in -tāt: adhástāt, avástāt, parástāt, purástāt. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVI, section 1100.)
- paçcā́t 'behind' is an ablative adverb. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter XVI, section 1114.)

## Unit 33: `vsn-animals` (animals), 6 of 6 words

| # | Headword | Stem or root | Part of speech | Gloss | Attested at (sa.wikisource page, verse; line no. in the verse) | The line | Griffith's English (whole verse, en.wikisource page) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | वृकः | वृक | noun (m) | wolf | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.४२ verse 2, line 1; attested as वृको | यो नः पूषन्नघो वृको दुःशेव आदिदेशति | Drive, Pusan, from our road the wolf, the wicked inauspicious wolf, Who lies in wait to injure us. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 42] |
| 2 | श्येनः | श्येन | noun (m) | hawk | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ९.६२ verse 4, line 2; attested as श्येनो | श्येनो न योनिमासदत् | Strong, mountain-born, the stalk hath been pressed in the streams for rapturous joy: Hawk-like he settles in his home. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 9/Hymn 62] |
| 3 | सिंहः | सिंह | noun (m) | lion | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ३.२ verse 11, line 1; attested as सिंहः | स जिन्वते जठरेषु प्रजज्ञिवान्वृषा चित्रेषु नानदन्न सिंहः | He stirs with life in wombs dissimilar in kind, born as a Lion or a loudly-bellowing Bull: Vaisvanara immortal with wide-reaching might, bestowing goods and wealth on him who offers gifts. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 3/Hymn 2] |
| 4 | मृगः | मृग | noun (m) | wild beast, deer | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं १.३८ verse 5, line 1; attested as मृगो | मा वो मृगो न यवसे जरिता भूदजोष्यः | Then never were your praiser loathed like a wild beast in pasture-land, Nor should he go on Yama's path. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 1/Hymn 38] |
| 5 | हंसः | हंस | noun (m) | swan, goose | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ९.३२ verse 3, line 1; attested as हंसो | आदीं हंसो यथा गणं विश्वस्यावीवशन्मतिम् | Now like a swan he maketh all the company sing each his hymn: He, like a steed, is bathed in milk. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 9/Hymn 32] |
| 6 | मण्डूकः | मण्डूक | noun (m) | frog | ऋग्वेदः सूक्तं ७.१०३ verse 4, line 2; attested as मण्डूको | मण्डूको यदभिवृष्टः कनिष्कन्पृश्निः सम्पृङ्क्ते हरितेन वाचम् | Each of these twain receives the other kindly, while they are revelling in the flow of waters, When the Frog moistened by the rain springs forward, and Green and Spotty both combine their voices. [The Hymns of the Rigveda/Book 7/Hymn 103] |

**Grammar notes a card can carry** (each from a Whitney page read):

- Among nouns, the a-stems (devá, kā́ma) have no irregularities. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter V, section 330, 331.)
- Final -as before a sonant becomes -o. (Sanskrit Grammar (Whitney)/Chapter III, section 175 a.)
